#+end_reasoning

Structurally, yes — that's the entire reason to be before-queue rather than a pipe. Postfix's own milter doc states the ordering plainly: header/body content is passed to the milter and it "may modify or reject that content /before/ it stores that content in the queue file." So when Husk encrypts at =eom=, cleanup writes ciphertext. There is no window in which a plaintext message file exists in your spool. The content lives in smtpd/libmilter memory, is rewritten, and only then becomes a queue file.

That is a genuine, categorical improvement over the =content_filter= design from earlier in this thread, where cleartext necessarily hits disk first and is encrypted on a second pass.

But "the plaintext never reaching the disk" is a much larger claim than "the queue file contains ciphertext," and the gap is where the honest answer lives.

/What "correctly" has to mean, or you get cleartext anyway/

- /Every recipient that can occur must be encryptable./ Husk decides per recipient. Role addresses (=postmaster@=, =abuse@=, =MAILER-DAEMON@=) and any address you forgot are not in =locals=, so mail to them is delivered in the clear, to disk, with Husk running perfectly. For a single-key setup this is the main operational hole: your key has one UID, and Husk matches certificates to recipient addresses.
- /No split./ If one recipient is encryptable and another isn't, the injected second message is plaintext by design.
- /No fail-open at the daemon level./ =milter_default_action = tempfail=, or a crash in Husk means Postfix accepts and stores cleartext.
- /Verified, not assumed./ =husk monitor readout= showing =unencrypted_rcpt: 0= and =encryption_error: 0= for real traffic. Configured-correctly is an observation, not a config file.

/Residual disk paths that aren't Husk's, and can't be configured away/

- /Internally generated mail./ Postfix deliberately does not filter bounces and Postmaster notifications — the same doc explains why (filtering a bounce can lose the mail, since a failed double-bounce has nowhere to go). Those are generated and queued locally, so their content, including quoted original headers and sometimes bodies in DSNs, is written unencrypted. =internal_mail_filter_classes = bounce= exists to bring them in, at the cost of that risk.
- /Anything else in the pipeline that touches the message./ A spam/AV filter, an archiver, or server-side FTS running before or after Husk will hand plaintext to software that routinely writes temp files. Encryption at =eom= protects the queue file, not every process that ever saw the bytes. Ordering and "who else reads this message" is part of your threat model, not part of Husk's config.
- /Swap, hibernation, core dumps./ Cleartext exists in smtpd and Husk memory by necessity. Any of those three writes it to disk. Encrypted swap or no swap, if you care.
- /Husk's own logs./ Debug-level logging is where people accidentally get message content into journald. Keep it at info.

/And the disclaimer that matters more than all of the above/

Even a perfect Husk deployment means: cleartext existed in the sender's MUA, the sender's Sent folder, the sending MTA's queue, and every hop between — and on your provider's network and any VPS snapshots taken before delivery. Husk encrypts /at the end of transport/, which protects the mailbox at rest from that moment forward. It does not make the message "never unencrypted." Nothing that runs on your server can.

So: /yes to "no plaintext in your mail store," no to "no plaintext on your disk," and definitely no to "no plaintext anywhere."/ The precise guarantee is "from the moment of acceptance, the only copy your machine persists is ciphertext" — which is exactly the property you wanted back when this started, and it's worth being able to state it that narrowly.

#+begin_reasoning
The user asks a precise question: on the recipient side filesystem only, will Husk be enough to not store unencrypted mail anywhere on the filesystem?

Answer: No. Several reasons:

1. Queue file — yes, encrypted if Husk encrypts. But there are paths where plaintext is written:
   - Held/deferred queue: if Husk tempfails, does Postfix store? Actually if milter tempfails at eom, the message is NOT stored — smtpd returns 451 and discards. Actually before-queue: content arrives at smtpd, passed to milter incrementally. Postfix stores content in cleanup only after milter accepts. On tempfail at EOM, Postfix returns 4xx and does not queue. So that's actually fine. But: Postfix cleanup writes the queue file... Let me think about this carefully.

Actually before-queue filtering in Postfix: smtpd receives DATA, streams to cleanup? No — smtpd passes headers/body to milters, then to cleanup which writes to queue file. The flow: smtpd collects the message and sends it to cleanup via a record stream. The milter callbacks happen in smtpd. Actually the milter protocol is spoken by smtpd for SMTP mail. smtpd buffers the message, and when milter returns CONTINUE at EOM, smtpd passes the (modified) content to cleanup. So yes, no queue file until milter done. However, there's a caveat: Postfix's milter content is buffered in memory, and cleanup writes to queue. So if milter accepts but modifies, only modified content goes to queue.

BUT — important caveat: with milter, Postfix's smtpd holds the message in memory (there's a limit; message size limit). Also, Postfix writes to queue after milter. Fine.

2. The big ones: internally generated mail (bounces, DSNs, Postmaster notifications) bypass milters by default → plaintext on disk. This is a real filesystem-level plaintext.

3. Postfix's "hold" queue — no, still after milter.

4. If milter_default_action=accept or daemon down → plaintext stored.

5. If the recipient isn't encryptable → plaintext stored.

6. Also: does Postfix keep a copy in the "corrupt" queue or deferred? If Husk encrypts, the queued copy is ciphertext. But the plaintext... not.

7. Another important one: milter_content_timeout / progress — no disk.

8. Also: smtpd's own queue? No.

9. postsuper -r re-injection re-runs cleanup but not milters for SMTP? Actually postsuper -r re-injects through cleanup with milters simulated (non_smtpd_milters). Hmm, for requeued messages cleanup simulates SMTP and applies non_smtpd_milters. If Husk is in non_smtpd_milters too (as docs recommend $smtpd_milters), then requeued ciphertext would be re-processed → is it detected as already encrypted → passthrough. OK.

10. Logs — Postfix logs don't include body. But Husk logs at debug.

11. Journald / systemd coredumps.

12. Any local delivery agent, IMAP server, Maildir, indexer... wait — on the recipient side, the mail is stored in the mailbox. If mail is encrypted at SMTP time, then the mailbox contains ciphertext. But: what about the recipient's own MUA saving a decrypted copy? That's downstream, and the user said "on the recipient" — hmm, "recipient side filesystem" could include the client. But they mean the mail server presumably. Let me address both: server-side is one thing; client-side (notmuch/mu, mbsync maildir) — if decrypted for reading, the plaintext appears in the local Maildir or index. Actually no — notmuch show --decrypt decrypts to stdout, doesn't write plaintext maildir file. But the index stores cleartext if index.decrypt=true. And session keys stashed.

Also: the MUA cache, temp files.

13. Also importantly: **Postfix's smtpd proxy / before-queue** - fine.

14. **The "incoming" queue vs "active"**: same file. Fine.

15. **TLS/memory**: covered before.

16. **Mailbox storage in Maildir/IMAP**: LMTP or lmtp to Dovecot gets ciphertext.

But wait — one more important filesystem-level path: **Postfix's message size limit and the "smtpd" buffering to a temp file**? Postfix does not spool to temp file during SMTP receipt in smtpd... Actually Postfix smtpd does spool message content to a queue file only via cleanup after DATA completes and milter accepts. Hmm, but there's a subtlety: Postfix smtpd writes to cleanup progressively via cleanup's record stream? Let me think: Postfix architecture — smtpd receives DATA, and forwards message content to cleanup(8); cleanup writes to queue file. Milters are invoked by smtpd before passing to cleanup. Actually the milter docs say "If Postfix does not reject the header/body content, Postfix passes it to the first configured Milter which may modify or reject that content." — and "All this happens before mail is queued." So milter sees content before cleanup writes queue file. Yes.

But: does smtpd hold entire message in memory? Postfix smtpd streams to cleanup as it receives, BUT milters require smtpd to buffer? Actually libmilter sends headers/body to the milter as they arrive; smtpd must buffer the content until EOM to apply modifications. Postfix does buffer in memory (there's a known memory cost of before-queue filtering: CONTENT_INSPECTION_README discusses memory). So plaintext in RAM.

So the answer to "will Husk be enough to not store unencrypted mail on the filesystem anywhere on the recipient?" → No, definitively not, because:

(a) Bounces/DSNs/Postmaster notifications are never filtered → plaintext on disk. This is guaranteed to happen if you send any mail that bounces. Quoted original message in DSN includes headers + optionally body.

(b) Milter failure paths (default_action=accept), daemon down before start, crash → plaintext.

(c) Non-encryptable recipients (role accounts, any address without cert) → plaintext by design.

(d) Any other software in the pipeline that touch mail: spam filter temp files, archiver, AV, mail archiving, MTA-level logging, backup snapshots of the queue, and MUAs/indexers.

(e) Mail you receive at a domain whose local delivery writes... no.

(f) Sent mail: Maildir Sent folder of the local MUA if the recipient also sends mail. Outbound via non_smtpd_milters could encrypt if cert available; else plaintext Sent copy. That's on the recipient-side filesystem.

(g) Local submission/cron mail from the server itself (root's cron, system notifications) — goes through pickup → non_smtpd_milters. If configured, and recipient not encryptable → plaintext in /var/mail or Maildir. On the recipient side, that's plaintext mail on disk. Cron output, logwatch, etc. Very common!

(h) Postfix's own `local` delivery to /var/mail: if someone sends to a local Unix account, and that address has no cert → plaintext.

(i) Also **the queue file of messages that are "held"**: with `postsuper -h`, they're already filtered. Fine.

(j) **Postfix "corrupt" queue / cleanup temp files**: does cleanup write a temp file and then rename? Postfix cleanup writes queue file directly with a temp name then renames. If the content is encrypted by milter, fine.

(k) **backups/rotation of logs, journald with debug**.

(l) **Core dumps** of smtpd holding plaintext — writes to disk.

(m) **Swap**.

So the honest answer: No, and it cannot be, because the guarantee "no plaintext email file anywhere on the filesystem" requires the encryption to be the only path by which any mail content can be persisted, and Husk is one filter among many in a system that has many other writers: DSN generation, local delivery, other filters, indexes, and the MTA's own failure modes.

I should also mention: the specific case of "already encrypted mail" being passed through — that's fine.

And a nuance: is bounces containing original content? Yes, DSNs include the original headers (and for some, the full message). Postfix's bounce includes headers of the failed message. So plaintext headers on disk. That's a real leak of subject/from/to — the metadata they wanted to hide (protected headers).

Also note that Husk's protected-subject feature only hides subject in the encrypted copy; the DSN quoting headers would leak it.

Also: if a message is encrypted and later the user runs something like notmuch/mbsync, fine.

Also worth noting: the earlier concern in the conversation - the user's actual goal seems to be "no unencrypted mail on the recipient filesystem." Even full success means metadata (envelope, subject outer for non-protected, timestamps, sizes, and mail logs) persists.

Also important: **Postfix logs** store sender/recipient/subject? Postfix logs sender, recipient, size, queue id, status — not subject. So metadata.

Also **the `mailq`/spool backups**.

Let me also mention that a stronger architecture: encrypt at the MUA/end-to-end, or use disk encryption for the whole filesystem, which is the only way to make the claim hold — full-disk encryption covers all the paths Husk can't (swap, core, DSNs, indexes). And that's the honest architectural point: Husk reduces the attack surface to "encrypted at rest from acceptance", but the property "no plaintext anywhere on disk" is a property of the filesystem, not of the MTA — so FDE + encrypted swap is the tool for that, and Husk is complementary, not sufficient.

That's a nice abstract insight: you're asking a policy question of the wrong layer. "Nothing unencrypted on this disk" is an OS-level invariant. "The mail store holds ciphertext" is an MTA-level invariant. Husk gives you the latter.

Let me structure the answer:

1. Direct answer: No. Two classes of failure: (i) Husk declines, (ii) Husk never sees it.
2. Class (i): recipient not encryptable, daemon down/accept, split, error cert. Within the mail-flow path — configurable-ish, mostly.
3. Class (ii): mail Husk never sees — DSNs/bounces, local cron mail, non-SMTP local delivery, mail submitted on unfiltered service, already-filtered requeue paths. This is the real answer to "anywhere on the filesystem."
4. Also non-mail writers: other filters' temp files, indexers, MUA Sent copies, logs at debug, core dumps, swap, backups/snapshots (of local disk - they said ignore provider snapshots; local backups still count).
5. Conclusion: Husk gives "the queue and mailbox hold ciphertext." It cannot give "no plaintext on this filesystem." That requires FDE + encrypted swap; and even then plaintext exists at rest decrypted while running. And the true end-to-end property requires the sender to encrypt.
6. What to actually do: Husk + FDE + milter_default_action=tempfail + internal_mail_filter_classes=bounce (with caveat) + monitor counters + minimal logging. But be honest that the enumeration of "anywhere" is unbounded.

Keep it concise-ish but substantive. Use the format from prior turns. Let me write it.

One more check: the claim "bounces are not filtered by milters" — MILTER_README says "Postfix normally does not apply content filters to mail that is generated internally such as bounces or Postmaster notifications." And internal_mail_filter_classes=bounce enables it. Confirmed from the fetched doc. Good.

Also note: even with internal_mail_filter_classes=bounce, a bounce for a non-encryptable recipient stays plaintext, and bounces have null/double-bounce sender so they can't be encrypted to a key meaningfully.

Also: local(8) delivery — for a domain in mydestination, mail goes to local(8) which writes /var/mail or Maildir. Still, content is whatever was queued (ciphertext if encrypted). But the address must have a cert. Fine.

Let me write the answer. Length: medium-long, tables/bullets, structured. Emphasize the layer mismatch insight.
#+end_reasoning

No. And the reason is structural, not configurational: Husk decides whether to encrypt /one message that reaches it/. Your question is about /every write the filesystem performs/. Those aren't the same set, and no milter can close the difference.

Split the failures into two kinds.

/1. Husk saw the message and declined/

These are the ones from the last answer, and they're the ones you can partly engineer away:

- recipient has no authenticated certificate (role addresses, aliases, anything not in =locals=)
- one encryptable + one not → injected plaintext twin
- cert expired/revoked/weak → =encryption_error=, behavior undocumented
- daemon down or crashed, with =milter_default_action = accept=
- listener you forgot to attach the milter to

Each of these ends in a plaintext file in the queue or the mailbox.

/2. Husk will never see the message — and this is the real answer/

- /DSNs and bounces./ Postfix deliberately exempts internally generated mail from filtering, for the reason its own doc gives: filtering a bounce can lose the message, because a failed double-bounce has nowhere to go. So every bounce writes a file containing the original headers — and often quoted body — in the clear. Note what that leaks: the =Subject: You were charged...= you just hid behind Husk's =subject_replacement= reappears, in plaintext, in the DSN on disk.
- /Locally generated mail./ Cron, logwatch, systemd failure notices, =mail(1)= from scripts. These enter via =pickup=, hit =non_smtpd_milters= if you configured it, and — with no cert for =root@= — land in =/var/mail/root= in the clear. On a normal server this is a continuous trickle of plaintext mail.
- /Local delivery generally./ Anything in =mydestination= written by =local(8)= is address-based; encryptability is per-address, and local account names won't have certs.
- /Postfix's own queue mechanics./ Held messages, deferred queue copies, =postsuper -r= re-injection, the corrupt queue — all are content paths Husk doesn't arbitrate.

/3. Not mail flow at all, but still your filesystem/

- Other filters in the chain: spam/AV scanners, archivers, server-side full-text indexers — these routinely write temp files.
- Husk's own logs at debug level.
- Your MUA's Sent copies, if you send from that machine; and any local index that stores cleartext (notmuch =index.decrypt=true=, which the previous turn flagged).
- Core dumps of smtpd or Husk, both of which hold plaintext in memory by necessity.
- Swap.
- Your own local backups and snapshots — you excluded the /provider's/, but a =borg=/=restic= target of that machine captures everything above.

/The layer mismatch/

"The mail store contains only ciphertext" is an MTA-level invariant, and Husk can plausibly deliver it. "Nothing unencrypted is ever written to this filesystem" is an OS-level invariant, and it is only deliverable by the filesystem: full-disk encryption plus encrypted swap. Those are complementary, not alternatives — FDE covers the DSNs, the cron mail, the core dump, and the temp file, which is precisely the set Husk structurally cannot reach.

And even the narrow claim has an honest edge: the guarantee is "ciphertext from the moment of acceptance." The message necessarily exists decrypted in the sender's MUA, in transit, and in RAM while smtpd is handing it to Husk. If what you actually want is "only I can read my mail," that property has exactly one source — the sender encrypting to your key — and Husk is then an optimization for mail that arrives unencrypted, not a substitute for it.

/If you want to get as close as the architecture allows/

=locals= with your one cert, no introducers · =milter_default_action = tempfail= · =internal_mail_filter_classes = bounce= (accepting the loss-of-bounce risk) · attach to every listener including submission · logs at info or above · =encrypted_rcpt= → =unencrypted_rcpt= → =encryption_error= monitored and expected to be zero for your address · plus FDE and encrypted swap. That gets you "the queue and the mailbox hold nothing readable," which is a real and useful property. It does not get you "nothing readable on the disk," and no configuration of Husk will.

for a release mismatch between NixOS mailserver and Nixpkgs.\n\nUsing mismatched versions is likely to cause compatibility issues and may require migrations that make an eventual rollback tricky.\n\nIt is therefore highly recommended to use a release of NixOS mailserver that corresponds with your chosen release of Nixpkgs.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.enablePop3<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.enablePop3\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n\nWhether to enable POP3 with STARTTLS on port on port 110.\n\nThe use of this port is deprecated per RFC 8314 4.1.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.enablePop3Ssl<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.enablePop3Ssl\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n\nWhether to enable POP3 with TLS in wrapper-mode on port 995.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.enableSubmission<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.enableSubmission\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n\nWhether to enable SMTP with STARTTLS on port 587.\n\nThe use of this port is discouraged per RFC 8314 3.3, see also Appendix A.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.enableSubmissionSsl<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.enableSubmissionSsl\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>true</code>\n\nWhether to enable SMTP with TLS in wrapper-mode on port 465.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.forwards<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.forwards\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>attribute set of ((list of string) or string)</code>\n- default: <code>{ }</code>\n- example:\n\n  ```\n  {\n    \"gamenight@example.com\" = [\n      \"bob@example.com\"\n      \"frank@example.org\"\n      \"wendy@example.net\"\n    ];\n    \"user@example.com\" = \"user@example.edu\";\n  }\n\n  ```\n\nForwards route mail from local addresses to one or more local or external addresses.\n\nUnlike <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.aliases\"><code>mailserver.aliases</code></a>, the target addresses cannot send mail using the forward address.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.fqdn<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.fqdn\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- example: <code>\"mx.example.com\"</code>\n\nThe fully qualified domain name of the mail server.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.hierarchySeparator<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.hierarchySeparator\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default: <code>\".\"</code>\n\nThe hierarchy separator for mailboxes used by dovecot for the namespace 'inbox'. Dovecot defaults to \".\" but recommends \"/\". This affects how mailboxes appear to mail clients and sieve scripts. For instance when using \".\" then in a sieve script \"<a href=\"http://example.com\">example.com</a>\" would refer to the mailbox \"com\" in the parent mailbox \"example\". This does not determine the way your mails are stored on disk. See <a href=\"https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/config/namespaces.html#namespaces\">https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/config/namespaces.html#namespaces</a> for details.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.imapMemoryLimit<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.imapMemoryLimit\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>signed integer</code>\n- default: <code>256</code>\n\nThe memory limit for the imap service, in megabytes.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.indexDir<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.indexDir\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or string</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>\"/var/lib/dovecot/indices\"</code>\n\nFolder to store search indices. If null, indices are stored along with email, which could not necessarily be desirable, especially when <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.fullTextSearch.enable\"><code>mailserver.fullTextSearch.enable</code></a> is <code>true</code> since indices it creates are voluminous and do not need to be backed up.\n\nBe careful when changing this option value since all indices would be recreated at the new location (and clients would need to resynchronize).\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.lmtpMemoryLimit<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.lmtpMemoryLimit\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>signed integer</code>\n- default: <code>256</code>\n\nThe memory limit for the LMTP service, in megabytes.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.lmtpSaveToDetailMailbox<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.lmtpSaveToDetailMailbox\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>true</code>\n\nIf an email address is delimited by a \"+\", should it be filed into a mailbox matching the string after the \"+\"? For example, <a href=\"mailto:user1+test%40example.com\">user1+test@example.com</a> would be filed into the mailbox \"test\".\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.localDnsResolver<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.localDnsResolver\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>true</code>\n\nRuns a local DNS resolver (kresd) as recommended when running rspamd. This prevents your log file from filling up with rspamd\\_monitored\\_dns\\_mon entries.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.mailboxes<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.mailboxes\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>unspecified value</code>\n- default:\n\n  ```\n  {\n    Drafts = {\n      auto = \"subscribe\";\n      special_use = \"\\\\Drafts\";\n    };\n    Junk = {\n      auto = \"subscribe\";\n      fts_autoindex = false;\n      special_use = \"\\\\Junk\";\n    };\n    Sent = {\n      auto = \"subscribe\";\n      special_use = \"\\\\Sent\";\n    };\n    Trash = {\n      auto = \"no\";\n      fts_autoindex = false;\n      special_use = \"\\\\Trash\";\n    };\n  }\n\n  ```\n\nThe default mailboxes for Dovecot maildirs.\n\nThe \\[<code>special_use</code>] option must refer to an <a href=\"https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc6154.html#section-2\">RFC6154 2</a> attribute and lead with an escaped backslash.\n\nDepending on the mail client used it might be necessary to change some mailbox's name.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.maxConnectionsPerUser<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.maxConnectionsPerUser\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>signed integer</code>\n- default: <code>100</code>\n\nMaximum number of IMAP/POP3 connections allowed for a user from each IP address. E.g. a value of 50 allows for 50 IMAP and 50 POP3 connections at the same time for a single user.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.messageSizeLimit<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.messageSizeLimit\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>signed integer</code>\n- default: <code>20971520</code>\n- example: <code>52428800</code>\n\nMessage size limit enforced by Postfix.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.openFirewall<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.openFirewall\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>true</code>\n\nAutomatically open ports in the firewall.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.recipientDelimiter<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.recipientDelimiter\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default: <code>\"+\"</code>\n\nConfigure the recipient delimiter.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.rejectRecipients<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.rejectRecipients\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>list of string</code>\n- default: <code>[ ]</code>\n- example:\n\n  ```\n  [\n    \"sales@example.com\"\n    \"info@example.com\"\n  ]\n\n  ```\n\nReject emails addressed to these local addresses from unauthorized senders. Use if a spammer has found email addresses in a catchall domain but you do not want to disable the catchall.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.rejectSender<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.rejectSender\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>list of string</code>\n- default: <code>[ ]</code>\n- example:\n\n  ```\n  [\n    \"example.com\"\n    \"spammer@example.net\"\n  ]\n\n  ```\n\nReject emails from these addresses from unauthorized senders. Use if a spammer is using the same domain or the same sender over and over.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.rejectSenderMessage<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.rejectSenderMessage\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default: <code>\"\"</code>\n- example: <code>\"Your e-mail has not been delivered because we have blocked your e-mail address. If you believe that your e-mail address has been blocked by mistake, or if you have any other legitimate concern, please contact &lt;address&gt;.\"</code>\n\nSMTP message returned to rejected senders. If not set the Postfix default will be used.\n\nThe message must be a single line and typically much shorter than 512 characters.\n\nThis could for example be used to provide a contact method (postal address, phone or alternative email) so rejected senders can exercise their <a href=\"https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2016/679/oj/eng#:~:text=Article%2021\">Art. 21 GDPR</a> right to object.\n\nIt is good practice to inform senders in advance that their email addresses may be processed for this purpose in accordance with <a href=\"https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2016/679/oj/eng#:~:text=Article%2013\">Art. 13 GDPR</a>. Storing their mail address for this purpose is generally regarded as a legitimate interest.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.rewriteMessageId<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.rewriteMessageId\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n\nRewrites the Message-ID's hostname-part of outgoing emails to the FQDN. Please be aware that this may cause problems with some mail clients relying on the original Message-ID.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.sendingFqdn<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.sendingFqdn\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default: <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.fqdn\"><code>mailserver.fqdn</code></a>\n- example: <code>\"myserver.example.com\"</code>\n\nThe fully qualified domain name of the mail server used to identify with remote servers.\n\nIf this server's IP serves purposes other than a mail server, it may be desirable for the server to have a name other than that to which the user will connect. For example, the user might connect to <a href=\"http://mx.example.com\">mx.example.com</a>, but the server's IP has reverse DNS that resolves to <a href=\"http://myserver.example.com\">myserver.example.com</a>; in this scenario, some mail servers may reject or penalize the message.\n\nThis setting allows the server to identify as <a href=\"http://myserver.example.com\">myserver.example.com</a> when forwarding mail, independently of <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.fqdn\"><code>mailserver.fqdn</code></a> (which, for SSL reasons, should generally be the name to which the user connects).\n\nSet this to the name to which the sending IP's reverse DNS resolves.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.stateVersion<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.stateVersion\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or (positive integer, meaning &gt;0)</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n\nTracking stateful version changes as an incrementing number.\n\nWhen a new release comes out we may require manual migration steps to be completed, before the new version can be put into production.\n\nIf your <code>stateVersion</code> is too low one or multiple assertions may trigger to give you instructions on what migrations steps are required to continue. Increase the <code>stateVersion</code> as instructed by the assertion message.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.systemContact<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.systemContact\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- example: <code>\"postmaster@example.com\"</code>\n\nThe email address where the administrative contact for this mail server is reachable.\n\nCurrently, this is only required when one of the following features is enabled:\n\n- SMTP TLS reports (<code>mailserver.tlsrpt.enable</code>)\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.systemDomain<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.systemDomain\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default:\n\n  ```\n  if config.networking.domain != null && lib.elem config.networking.domain cfg.domains then\n    config.networking.domain\n  else\n    lib.head cfg.domains\n\n  ```\n\n- example: <code>config.networking.domain</code>\n\nThe primary domain used for sending automated reports.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.systemName<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.systemName\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default: <code>${config.mailserver.systemDomain} mail system</code>\n- example: <code>\"ACME Corp.\"</code>\n\nThe sender name given in automated reports.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.useUTF8FolderNames<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.useUTF8FolderNames\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n\nStore mailbox names on disk using UTF-8 instead of modified UTF-7 (mUTF-7).\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.virusScanning<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.virusScanning\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n\nWhether to activate virus scanning. Note that virus scanning is *very* expensive memory wise.\n\n</dd></dl>\n\n## `mailserver.accounts`\n\n<dl><dt>mailserver.accounts<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>attribute set of (submodule)</code>\n- default: <code>{ }</code>\n- example:\n\n  ```\n  {\n    user1 = {\n      # This password hash leaks into the Nix store\n      hashedPassword = \"$y$j9T$y6eZ1o.IvVNfdGMAsUEvh1$6K/llP52uw2iDh4iSwtAn54/JYy7FzCcoCHmjmx00H5\";\n    };\n    user2 = {\n      # Hashed password passed as a file\n      hashedPasswordFile = \"/run/keys/user2-pw-hash\";\n    };\n    user3 = {\n      # Plaintext password file\n      passwordFile = \"/run/keys/user3-pw\";\n    };\n  }\n\n  ```\n\nAttribute set of mail accounts.\n\nEach entry defines a mailbox and login credentials, where the attribute name is used as the login username and optionally routed mail address.\n\nUse <code>mkpasswd</code> to generate password hashes.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.aliases<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.aliases\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>list of string</code>\n- default: <code>[ ]</code>\n- example:\n\n  ```\n  [\n    \"abuse@example.com\"\n    \"postmaster@example.com\"\n  ]\n\n  ```\n\nList of additional mail addresses (aliases) that get routed to this account.\n\nCatch-all with sending permissions\n\nConfigure <code>@example.com</code> to create a catch-all for this domain that also allows sending from all addresses.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.aliasesRegexp<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.aliasesRegexp\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>list of string</code>\n- default: <code>[ ]</code>\n- example:\n\n  ```\n  [\n    \"/^tom\\\\..*@domain\\\\.com$/\"\n  ]\n\n  ```\n\nSame as <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.aliases\"><code>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.aliases</code></a> but using PCRE (Perl compatible regex).\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.catchAll<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.catchAll\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>list of value \"example.com\" (singular enum)</code>\n- default: <code>[ ]</code>\n- example:\n\n  ```\n  [\n    \"example.com\"\n    \"example2.com\"\n  ]\n\n  ```\n\nFor which domains should this account act as a catch all?\n\nWarning\n\nDoes not allow sending from all addresses of these domains. Use <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.aliases\"><code>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.aliases</code></a> if that is required.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.hashedPassword<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.hashedPassword\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or string</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>\"$y$j9T$vfGrwkAaXCjCEWtVNMQck1$383uIXQmn2z0hnmVAA8kwFQmjNj78.nYbvWeyNLIaP1\"</code>\n\nThe hashed login password for this account.\n\nUse <code>mkpasswd</code> to create password hashes:\n\n```\nnix-shell -p mkpasswd --run 'mkpasswd -s'\n\n```\n\nNote\n\nThis is a convenience option, when your threat model allows storing hashed secrets in the world-readable Nix store.\n\nPassing the hash through <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.hashedPasswordFile\"><code>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.hashedPasswordFile</code></a> allows relying on filesystem discretionary access control as another security boundary.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.hashedPasswordFile<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.hashedPasswordFile\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or absolute path</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>\"/run/keys/user1-pw-hash\"</code>\n\nThe hashed login password for this account read from a file.\n\nUse \\`mkpasswd to create password hashes:\n\n```\nnix-shell -p mkpasswd --run 'mkpasswd -s'\n\n```\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.passwordFile<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.passwordFile\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or path not in the Nix store</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>\"/run/keys/user1-pw\"</code>\n\nThe plaintext login password for this account read from a file.\n\nNote\n\nThe password is hashed before it is passed on to Dovecot.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.quota<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.quota\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or string</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>\"2G\"</code>\n\nThe quota limit for this user.\n\nThe value is must use a size format like <code>500M</code>, <code>2G</code>, <code>10G</code>.\n\nIf unset, will fall back to <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.quota.defaults.perUser\"><code>mailserver.quota.defaults.perUser</code></a> if set.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.sendOnly<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.sendOnly\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n\nSpecifies if the account should be a send-only account.\n\nEmails sent to send-only accounts will be rejected with the reason configured in <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.sendOnlyRejectMessage\"><code>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.sendOnlyRejectMessage</code></a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.sendOnlyRejectMessage<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.sendOnlyRejectMessage\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default: <code>\"This account cannot receive emails.\"</code>\n\nThe message returned to the sender for a send-only account.\n\nSee <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.sendOnly\"><code>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.sendOnly</code></a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.accounts.&lt;name&gt;.sieveScript<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.accounts.-name-.sieveScript\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or strings concatenated with \"\\n\"</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example:\n\n  ```\n  ''\n    require [\"fileinto\", \"mailbox\"];\n\n    if address :is \"from\" \"gitlab@mg.gitlab.com\" {\n      fileinto :create \"GitLab\";\n      stop;\n    }\n\n    # This must be the last rule, it will check if list-id is set, and\n    # file the message into the Lists folder for further investigation\n    elsif header :matches \"list-id\" \"<?*>\" {\n      fileinto :create \"Lists\";\n      stop;\n    }\n  ''\n\n  ```\n\nPer-user sieve script.\n\n</dd></dl>\n\n## `mailserver.x509`\n\n<dl><dt>mailserver.x509.certificateFile<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.x509.certificateFile\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or absolute path</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>\"/var/keys/certs/fullchain.pem\"</code>\n\nPath to the signed X509 certificate including intermediate certificates.\n\nThis is commonly referred to as <code>fullchain.pem</code>.\n\nMutually exclusive with <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.x509.useACMEHost\"><code>mailserver.x509.useACMEHost</code></a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.x509.privateKeyFile<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.x509.privateKeyFile\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or string</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>\"/var/keys/certs/privkey.pem\"</code>\n\nPath to the X509 private key.\n\nThis is commonly referred to as <code>privkey.pem</code>.\n\nMutually exclusive with <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.x509.useACMEHost\"><code>mailserver.x509.useACMEHost</code></a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.x509.useACMEHost<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.x509.useACMEHost\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or string</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>config.mailserver.fqdn</code>\n\nCommon name used in the relevant <code>security.acme.certs</code> configuration.\n\nMutually exclusive with <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.x509.certificateFile\"><code>mailserver.x509.certificateFile</code></a> and <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.x509.privateKeyFile\"><code>mailserver.x509.privateKeyFile</code></a>.\n\n</dd></dl>\n\n## `mailserver.storage`\n\n<dl><dt>mailserver.storage.directoryLayout<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.storage.directoryLayout\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>one of \"fs\", \"Maildir++\"</code>\n- default: <code>\"Maildir++\"</code>\n\nSets whether dovecot should organize mail in subdirectories:\n\n- /var/vmail/example.com/user/.folder.subfolder/ (Maildir++ layout)\n- /var/vmail/example.com/user/folder/subfolder/ (FS layout)\n\nSee <a href=\"https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/config/mailbox_formats/maildir.html#directory-layout\">https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/config/mailbox\\_formats/maildir.html#directory-layout</a> for further details.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.storage.gid<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.storage.gid\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>positive integer, meaning &gt;0</code>\n- default: <code>5000</code>\n\nThe group id of the primary group of the vmail user.\n\nThis group owns the mail storage directories. Access can be delegated to other users via group membership.\n\nWarning\n\nIf you change this value you also need to manually adjust the ownership of your <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.storage.path\"><code>mailserver.storage.path</code></a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.storage.group<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.storage.group\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default: <code>\"virtualMail\"</code>\n\nThe primary group name of the user that owns the <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.storage.path\"><code>mailserver.storage.path</code></a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.storage.owner<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.storage.owner\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default: <code>\"virtualMail\"</code>\n\nThe name of the user that owns the <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.storage.path\"><code>mailserver.storage.path</code></a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.storage.path<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.storage.path\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>absolute path</code>\n- default: <code>\"/var/vmail\"</code>\n\nPath on disk where mail home directories are stored.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.storage.uid<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.storage.uid\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>positive integer, meaning &gt;0</code>\n- default: <code>5000</code>\n\nThe user id assigned to the vmail user.\n\nThis user owns the mail storage files and directories and is used by services accessing the mail store.\n\nWarning\n\nIf you change this value you also need to manually adjust the ownership of your <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.storage.path\"><code>mailserver.storage.path</code></a>.\n\n</dd></dl>\n\n## `mailserver.dkim`\n\n<dl><dt>mailserver.dkim.defaults.keyLength<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.defaults.keyLength\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>signed integer</code>\n- default: <code>2048</code>\n\nThe default key length used for generating new DKIM keys.\n\nOnly applies for RSA keys, Ed25519 keys use a fixed key length.\n\nPer <a href=\"https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc8301#section-3.2\">RFC8301 3.2</a> the minimum RSA key length should be at least 2048 bit.\n\nThis value should most likely not be changed. Once DKIM keys for domain and selector are generated changing this value will not regenerate the keypair. Instead create a new selector and configure <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.domains.-name-.selectors.-name-.keyLength\"><code>mailserver.dkim.domains.&lt;name&gt;.selectors.&lt;name&gt;.keyLength</code></a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.dkim.defaults.keyType<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.defaults.keyType\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>one of \"rsa\", \"ed25519\"</code>\n- default: <code>\"rsa\"</code>\n\nThe key type used for generating DKIM keys. Ed25519 support was introduced in RFC6376 (2018).\n\nWarning\n\nEd25519 DKIM keys are currently not recommended for sole use, as various DKIM validators out there lack support and consider the keypair invalid.\n\nThis value should most likely not be changed. Once DKIM keys for domain and selector are generated changing this value will not regenerate the keypair. Instead create a new selector and configure <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.domains.-name-.selectors.-name-.keyType\"><code>mailserver.dkim.domains.&lt;name&gt;.selectors.&lt;name&gt;.keyType</code></a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.dkim.defaults.selector<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.defaults.selector\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default: <code>\"mail\"</code>\n\nThe default selector used to reference and lookup DKIM keys.\n\nThis value should most likely not be changed. Instead manage <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.domains.-name-.selectors\"><code>mailserver.dkim.domains.&lt;name&gt;.selectors</code></a> to sign with one or multiple DKIM key pairs and manage migrations.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.dkim.domains<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.domains\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>attribute set of (submodule)</code>\n- default: <code>{ }</code>\n- example:\n\n  ```\n  {\n    \"example.com\".selectors = {\n      \"mail\" = {\n        # inherit defaults from mailserver.dkim.defaults\n      };\n      \"rsa-2026-03\".keyFile = \"/run/keys/example.com-dkim-rsa-2026-03.key\";\n    };\n  };\n\n  ```\n\nDKIM configuration per domain.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.dkim.domains.&lt;name&gt;.selectors<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.domains.-name-.selectors\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>attribute set of (submodule)</code>\n- default: <code>{ }</code>\n- example:\n\n  ```\n  {\n    \"mail\" = {\n      # inherit defaults from mailserver.dkim.defaults\n    };\n    \"rsa-2026-03\".keyFile = \"/run/keys/example.com-dkim-rsa-2026-03.key\";\n  };\n\n  ```\n\nDKIM selectors used for signing outgoing mail for this domain.\n\nWhen no selector is configured a default selector will be created with settings inherited from <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.defaults.keyLength\"><code>mailserver.dkim.defaults</code></a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.dkim.domains.&lt;name&gt;.selectors.&lt;name&gt;.keyFile<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.domains.-name-.selectors.-name-.keyFile\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or path not in the Nix store</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>\"/run/keys/example.com-dkim-rsa-2026-03.key\"</code>\n\nPath to an existing DKIM private key file.\n\nDKIM keys can be generated using <code>rspamadm dkim_keygen</code>.\n\nThis option is mutually exclusive with <code>keyType</code> and <code>keyLength</code>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.dkim.domains.&lt;name&gt;.selectors.&lt;name&gt;.keyLength<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.domains.-name-.selectors.-name-.keyLength\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or signed integer</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>2048</code>\n\nThe key length used for generating this DKIM key.\n\nOnly applies for RSA keys, Ed25519 keys use a fixed key size.\n\nThis option is mutually exclusive with <code>keyFile</code>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.dkim.domains.&lt;name&gt;.selectors.&lt;name&gt;.keyType<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.domains.-name-.selectors.-name-.keyType\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or one of \"rsa\", \"ed25519\"</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>\"rsa\"</code>\n\nThe key type used for generating this DKIM keypair.\n\nWarning\n\nEd25519 DKIM keys are currently not recommended for sole use, as various DKIM validators out there lack support and consider the keypair invalid.\n\nThis option is mutually exclusive with <code>keyFile</code>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.dkim.enable<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.enable\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>true</code>\n- example: <code>true</code>\n\nWhether to enable DKIM signing.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.dkim.keyDirectory<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dkim.keyDirectory\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>absolute path</code>\n- default: <code>\"/var/dkim\"</code>\n\nThe path where DKIM siging keys are stored.\n\n</dd></dl>\n\n## `mailserver.srs`\n\n<dl><dt>mailserver.srs.domain<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.srs.domain\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or string</code>\n- default: <code>config.mailserver.systemDomain</code>\n- example: <code>\"srs.example.com\"</code>\n\nMail domain used for ephemeral SRS envelope addresses.\n\nNote\n\nThis domain can only support relaxed SPF alignment.\n\nImportant\n\nFor privacy reasons you should use a dedicated domain when serving multiple unrelated domains.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.srs.enable<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.srs.enable\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n- example: <code>true</code>\n\nWhether to enable Sender Rewrite Scheme.\n\n</dd></dl>\n\n## `mailserver.dmarcReporting`\n\n<dl><dt>mailserver.dmarcReporting.enable<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dmarcReporting.enable\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n\nWhether to send out aggregated, daily DMARC reports in response to incoming mail, when the sender domain defines a DMARC policy including the RUA tag.\n\nThis is helpful for the mail ecosystem, because it allows third parties to get notified about SPF/DKIM violations originating from their sender domains.\n\nSee <a href=\"https://rspamd.com/doc/modules/dmarc.html#reporting\">https://rspamd.com/doc/modules/dmarc.html#reporting</a>\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.dmarcReporting.excludeDomains<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.dmarcReporting.excludeDomains\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>list of string</code>\n- default: <code>[ ]</code>\n\nList of domains or eSLDs to be excluded from DMARC reports.\n\n</dd></dl>\n\n## `mailserver.tlsrpt`\n\n<dl><dt>mailserver.tlsrpt.enable<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.tlsrpt.enable\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n- example: <code>true</code>\n\nWhether to enable delivery of SMTP TLS reports according to RFC 8460.\n\n</dd></dl>\n\n## `mailserver.fullTextSearch`\n\n<dl><dt>mailserver.fullTextSearch.autoIndex<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.fullTextSearch.autoIndex\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>true</code>\n\nEnable automatic indexing of messages as they are received or modified.\n\nTip\n\nCan be overridden per mailbox by setting <code>fts_autoindex</code> for <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.mailboxes\"><code>mailserver.mailboxes</code></a>. By default the Junk and Trash folders are already excluded.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.fullTextSearch.enable<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.fullTextSearch.enable\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n- example: <code>true</code>\n\nWhether to enable Full text search indexing with Xapian through the fts\\_flatcurve plugin. This has significant performance and disk space cost. .\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.fullTextSearch.fallback<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.fullTextSearch.fallback\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>true</code>\n\nWhether to fallback to slow non-indexed search, if FTS lookup and indexing have failed.\n\nSee <a href=\"https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/plugins/fts.html#fts_search_read_fallback\">https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/plugins/fts.html#fts\\_search\\_read\\_fallback</a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.fullTextSearch.filters<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.fullTextSearch.filters\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>list of string</code>\n- default:\n\n  ```\n  [\n    \"normalizer-icu\"\n    \"snowball\"\n    \"stopwords\"\n  ]\n\n  ```\n\nThe list of <a href=\"https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/plugins/fts.html#filter-configuration\">language filters</a> to apply.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.fullTextSearch.headerExcludes<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.fullTextSearch.headerExcludes\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>list of string</code>\n- default:\n\n  ```\n  [\n    \"Received\"\n    \"DKIM-*\"\n    \"X-*\"\n    \"Comments\"\n  ]\n\n  ```\n\nThe list of headers to exclude while indexing.\n\nSee <a href=\"https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/plugins/fts.html#fts_header_excludes\">https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/plugins/fts.html#fts\\_header\\_excludes</a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.fullTextSearch.languages<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.fullTextSearch.languages\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>non-empty (list of string)</code>\n- default:\n\n  ```\n  [\n    \"en\"\n  ]\n\n  ```\n\n- example:\n\n  ```\n  [\n    \"en\"\n    \"de\"\n  ]\n\n  ```\n\nA list of languages that the full text search should detect.\n\nAt least one language must be specified. The language listed first is the default and is used when language recognition fails.\n\nSee <a href=\"https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/plugins/fts.html#languages\">https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/plugins/fts.html#languages</a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.fullTextSearch.memoryLimit<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.fullTextSearch.memoryLimit\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or signed integer</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>1024</code>\n\nMemory limit for the indexer process, in MiB.\n\nWhen <code>null</code> the \\[default\\_vsz\\_limit] applies while with <code>0</code> no limit is applied.\n\n<a href=\"https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/summaries/settings.html#default_vsz_limit\">default_vsz_limit</a>\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.fullTextSearch.substringSearch<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.fullTextSearch.substringSearch\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n\nWhether to allows substring searches. By default only prefix searches are supported.\n\nWarning\n\nEnabling this significantly increases storage requirements.\n\nSee <a href=\"https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/plugins/fts_flatcurve.html#fts_flatcurve_substring_search\">https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/plugins/fts\\_flatcurve.html#fts\\_flatcurve\\_substring\\_search</a>.\n\n</dd></dl>\n\n## `mailserver.quota`\n\n<dl><dt>mailserver.quota.defaults.perUser<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.quota.defaults.perUser\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or string</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>\"10G\"</code>\n\nDefault quota applied to all users.\n\nThe value must use a size format like <code>500M</code>, <code>2G</code>, <code>10G</code>.\n\nIf set to <code>null</code>, no default per user quota is applied and only explicit per user quotas apply, if set.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.quota.enable<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.quota.enable\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>true</code>\n\nWhether to enable quota support.\n\nWhen enabled, incoming mail can be rejected if a mailbox exceeds its quota.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.quotaStatusMemoryLimit<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.quotaStatusMemoryLimit\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>signed integer</code>\n- default: <code>256</code>\n\nThe memory limit for the quota-status service, in megabytes.\n\n</dd></dl>\n\n## `mailserver.redis`\n\n<dl><dt>mailserver.redis.address<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.redis.address\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default: <code>config.services.redis.servers.rspamd.unixSocket</code>\n\nPath, IP address or hostname that Rspamd should use to contact Redis.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.redis.configureLocally<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.redis.configureLocally\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>true</code>\n\nWhether to provision a local Redis instance.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.redis.password<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.redis.password\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or string</code>\n- default: <code>config.services.redis.servers.rspamd.requirePass</code>\n\nPassword that rspamd should use to contact redis, or null if not required.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.redis.port<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.redis.port\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or 16 bit unsigned integer; between 0 and 65535 (both inclusive)</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>config.services.redis.servers.rspamd.port</code>\n\nPort that Rspamd should use to contact Redis.\n\n</dd></dl>\n\n## `mailserver.ldap`\n\n<dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.attributes.mail<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.attributes.mail\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default: <code>\"mail\"</code>\n- example: <code>\"maildrop\"</code>\n\nThe attribute name used for looking up accounts by mail address.\n\nTypically this can be the <code>mail</code> attribute from the <code>inetOrgPerson</code> schema, or the <code>maildrop</code> attribute from the unofficial Postfix schema.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.attributes.password<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.attributes.password\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or string</code>\n- default: <code>null</code>\n- example: <code>\"userPassword\"</code>\n\nThe LDAP attribute referencing the account password used to login with.\n\nThe account passwords stored in LDAP must be hashed with a supported <a href=\"https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/config/auth/schemes.html\">Password Scheme</a> in order for Dovecot to understand them.\n\nTypically the <code>userPassword</code> attribute which is part of the <code>inetOrgPerson</code> schema.\n\nIf <code>null</code>, <a href=\"https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/config/auth/databases/ldap.html#authentication-binds\">Authentication Binds</a> will be used instead.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.attributes.username<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.attributes.username\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default: <code>\"uid\"</code>\n- example: <code>\"name\"</code>\n\nThe LDAP attribute referencing the username used to login with.\n\nTypically the <code>uid</code> attribute which is part of the <code>inetOrgPerson</code> schema.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.attributes.uuid<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.attributes.uuid\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default: <code>\"entryUUID\"</code>\n- example: <code>\"uuid\"</code>\n\nThe long-term stable LDAP attribute to reference accounts across username changes. Used to determine a stable Dovecot home and mail directory location.\n\nTypically the <code>entryUUID</code> attribute as defined by <a href=\"https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc4530.html\">RFC4530</a>.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.base<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.base\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- example: <code>\"ou=people,ou=accounts,dc=example,dc=com\"</code>\n\nBase DN below which user accounts are searched for.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.bind.dn<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.bind.dn\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- example: <code>\"cn=mail,ou=accounts,dc=example,dc=com\"</code>\n\nDN used to bind against the LDAP server.\n\nThe server uses this account to lookup and filter accounts.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.bind.passwordFile<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.bind.passwordFile\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>path not in the Nix store</code>\n- example: <code>\"/run/my-secret\"</code>\n\nFile containing the password required to bind against the LDAP server.\n\nWarning\n\nThe password file is read verbatim. Any trailing newline will become part of the password and may cause authentication failures.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.caFile<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.caFile\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>absolute path</code>\n- default: <code>${pkgs.cacert}/etc/ssl/certs/ca-bundle.crt</code>\n\nBundle of CA certificates used to authenticate the LDAP server certificate.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.dovecot.passFilter<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.dovecot.passFilter\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>null or string</code>\n- default:\n\n  ```\n  with config.mailserver.ldap.attributes; \"${username}=%{user}\";\n\n  ```\n\n- example: <code>\"(&amp;(memberOf=cn=mail_users,ou=groups,dc=example,dc=com)(uid=%{user}))\"</code>\n\nLDAP filter used to restrict which users are eligible to authenticate against Dovecot.\n\nSee the <a href=\"https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/summaries/settings.html#passdb_ldap_filter\">passdb\\_ldap\\_filter</a> reference in the Dovecot manual.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.dovecot.userFilter<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.dovecot.userFilter\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default:\n\n  ```\n  with config.mailserver.ldap.attributes; \"(|(${mail}=%{user})(${username}=%{user}))\";\n\n  ```\n\n- example: <code>\"(|(mail=%{user})(uid=%{user}))\"</code>\n\nLDAP filter used for LMTP delivery from Postfix and post-login information construction, like the home directory.\n\nSee the <a href=\"https://doc.dovecot.org/2.4.4/core/summaries/settings.html#userdb_ldap_filter\">userdb\\_ldap\\_filter</a> reference at in the Dovecot manual.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.enable<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.enable\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n- example: <code>true</code>\n\nWhether to enable LDAP support.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.postfix.filter<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.postfix.filter\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>string</code>\n- default:\n\n  ```\n  with config.mailserver.ldap.attributes; \"${mail}=%s\";\n\n  ```\n\n- example: <code>\"(mail=%s)\"</code>\n\nLDAP filter used to search for an account by mail, where <code>%s</code> is a substitute for the address in question.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.scope<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.scope\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>one of \"base\", \"one\", \"sub\"</code>\n- default: <code>\"sub\"</code>\n\nSearch scope relative to the <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.base\"><code>mailserver.ldap.base</code></a>.\n\n- base: Only the exact Base DN\n- one: Immediate child entries of the Base DN, but not the Base DN itself.\n- sub: Base DN and all descendant entries at any depth.\n\nIn practice only <code>one</code> or <code>sub</code> are suitable for multiple LDAP users.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.startTls<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.startTls\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>false</code>\n\nWhether to enable StartTLS on <code>ldap://</code> connections.\n\n</dd></dl><dl><dt>mailserver.ldap.uris<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.uris\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>list of string</code>\n- default: <code>[ ]</code>\n- example:\n\n  ```\n  [\n    \"ldaps://ldap1.example.com\"\n    \"ldaps://ldap2.example.com\"\n  ]\n\n  ```\n\nList of LDAP server URIs. Multiple can be specified.\n\nUse <code>ldaps://</code> for implicit TLS or <code>ldap://</code> for a plain connection. See also <a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.ldap.startTls\"><code>mailserver.ldap.startTls</code></a> to enable StartTLS on plain connections.\n\n</dd></dl>\n\n## `mailserver.workarounds`\n\n<dl><dt>mailserver.workarounds.all<a href=\"#cmdoption-arg-mailserver.workarounds.all\" title=\"Permalink to this definition\"></a></dt>\n<dd>\n\n- type: <code>boolean</code>\n- default: <code>true</code>\n\nWhether to enable various workarounds for compatibility with other mail servers or clients. This is safe to enable and workarounds come and go. At times, this option may have no effect at all.\n\n</dd></dl>"
}

No — and with SNM specifically, the answer gets worse before it gets better, because SNM ships a component that writes message metadata to disk before Husk ever sees the message.

/The structural limit you can't configure away/

Husk encrypts the MIME body. The RFC 5322 outer headers are necessarily cleartext — =From=, =To=, =Date=, =Message-ID=, =Received=, DKIM signatures — and =Subject= is only hidden by substitution (=...=). So every stored message is "ciphertext body with cleartext envelope headers." If your requirement is literally "no unencrypted mail on disk," PGP/MIME cannot satisfy it for any implementation. The goal has to be stated as "no readable body or subject," and even that loses to the next section.

/SNM-specific disk paths Husk doesn't cover/

- rspamd, and this is the big one. SNM enables it by default and wires it in as the milter, so it is already in your =smtpd_milters= chain, typically before Husk. rspamd records per-message metadata in the local Redis instance (=mailserver.redis.configureLocally= defaults true), and Redis persists to disk via RDB snapshots. That metadata includes subject-like fields. So the subject you just hid behind =subject_replacement= can be sitting in =/var/lib/redis-rspamd/= — verify with =redis-cli --scan= rather than trusting either of us. This is plaintext-on-disk that Husk architecturally cannot prevent, because it's a different program with its own storage.
- Milker ordering and wiring. SNM's module interface has no milter option at all; you're overriding =services.postfix.config.smtpd_milters= and appending Husk after rspamd, then reasoning about what each one does to the other's view of the body and DKIM. Get the order wrong and you sign ciphertext for outbound, or hand rspamd the plaintext and call it done.
- systemd-coredump. On by default on NixOS. A crashing smtpd or Husk dumps process memory — which by necessity contains cleartext — into =/var/lib/systemd/coredump=, compressed, unencrypted. This is the single most overlooked path on NixOS boxes and it's exactly the message you were protecting.
- Swap. Cleartext exists in RAM during the transaction. zram or no swap, otherwise it lands on disk.
- Journald. Default NixOS journald is volatile (=/run=, tmpfs) because =/var/log/journal= doesn't exist — good, and you should keep it that way or accept that Postfix/rspamd/dovecot logs then persist. Postfix itself doesn't log bodies or subjects, but rspamd and any debug-level Husk logging are the ones to check.
- DSNs and locally generated mail, unchanged from before. Postfix exempts internally generated bounces from filtering; bounces contain the original headers in the clear — including the real Subject. Cron/systemd mail to =root@= goes through =pickup= → =local(8)= → =/var/mail/root= unencrypted. SNM's virtual delivery doesn't change this path.
- Dovecot's own artifacts. Maildir files are ciphertext-bodied, but =dovecot.index/=, =dovecot-uidlist=, and any FTS index (=mailserver.fullTextSearch=, off by default) hold header data — again, the headers were never encrypted anyway.

/What actually closes the gap/

Full-disk encryption plus encrypted swap plus coredump disabled, and — if you want the header and index layer too — Dovecot's =mail_crypt= plugin, which encrypts the stored message as an opaque blob and is what an "encrypted at rest" mailstore really means. SNM doesn't expose it; you'd be hand-merging Dovecot config. Husk and =mail_crypt= are complements with different threat models: Husk gives you nothing-readable-for-whoever-reads-the-file, =mail_crypt= gives you nothing-readable-without-the-Dovecot-key, and only the latter covers the headers, the DSNs and the rspamd metadata.

/Verdict for your actual setup/

Correctly configured Husk with =locals=, =milter_default_action = tempfail=, verified =unencrypted_rcpt: 0=, gets you the strongest thing a milter can: the message body in your Maildir is ciphertext from the instant of acceptance, and never existed in plaintext in Postfix's queue. That is a real and meaningful property, and it's better than the =content_filter= design.

It does not get you "nothing unencrypted on the recipient filesystem." The three things that will still be there are cleartext headers (structural), rspamd's Redis metadata (SNM default), and anything a crash or swap writes out (OS layer). Two of those three are fixable — reorder or remove rspamd from the milter chain, and harden coredump/swap — and one isn't, ever, at this layer.

***
